10 月经济数据点评

以稳定的生产端应对需求端波动   
10 月经济数据整体偏弱，表现较好的仅有高技术产业工业增加值同比增速；
我们认为国庆长假前后居民休假，可能对 10 月工作日时间的影响较明显，进
而导致经济数据表现偏弱。 
◼ 10 月工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，从行业分类来看，1-10 月采矿业工业增
加值累计同比增速 1.9%，制造业累计同比增速 5.7%，公用事业累计同比
增速 5.3%，高技术产业累计同比增速 14.6%。 
◼ 10 月社零同比增长 0.4%，其中，除汽车以外的消费品零售额同比增长
2.5%，1-10 月服务消费累计同比增长 4.9%。 
◼ 1-10 月固定资产投资累计同比增速下降 8.1%，民间固定资产投资累计同
比下降 11.4%，扣除房地产开发投资，1-10 月全国固定资产投资下降 5.1%，
民间投资下降 7.8%。分主要行业看，制造业投资 1-10 月累计同比下降
2.9%，基建投资累计同比下降 7.3%，地产投资累计同比下降 21.6%。 
◼ 1-10 月商品房销售面积同比下降 13.7%，商品房销售额累计同比下降
14.3%。10 月，70 个大中城市新建商品住宅价格指数同比下降 2.7%，较
9 月上升 0.3 个百分点，二手住宅价格指数下降 4.4%，较 9 月上升 0.4 个
百分点。 
◼ 10 月经济数据偏弱。工业增加值同比增速较 9 月下行 0.7 个百分点，且
低于万得一致预期，社零同比增速较 9 月下行 0.7 个百分点，且低于万得
一致预期，1-10 月固定资产投资累计同比增速较 1-9 月下行 0.9 个百分点，
且低于万得一致预期。从细项看，10 月经济数据中表现较好的是工业增
加值中的高技术产业，以及依托外需的出口交货值，此前经济数据中表
现较好的部分，如固定资产投资中的设备购置累计同比增速、社零中的
通信工具当月同比增速，在 10 月也都出现了增速下降的情况。另一方面，
此前偏弱的数据尚未出现好转迹象，包括房地产固定资产投资增速和商
品房销售数据同比增速仍在下降，基建投资增速仍在下滑等等。考虑到
今年的国庆和中秋假期相邻，部分居民选择利用年假增加国庆前后出行
时间，我们认为 10 月经济数据偏弱，一定程度上可能有假期安排对工作
日天数缩短的影响。 
◼ 关注 2027 年经济增长目标设定。12 月政治局会议时间一般在上旬，这就
意味着 10 月经济数据对于定调 2027 年经济工作的影响较大。从 10 月数
据的特点来看，我们认为较为确定的几个方向：一是三驾马车中净出口
仍将是 2027 年经济增长的重要拉动力，二是消费偏弱的状态大概率将延
续，三是基建投资增速仍较大程度上依赖财政支出，短期内的亮点可能
是“六张网”，四是房地产行业即便有政策支持，整体或仍在寻底的过
程中，连带房地产产业链上下游中期内都将处于寻底过程，五是高技术
产业或将保持较好增速，六是国内通胀压力整体处于偏低水平，七是适
度宽松的货币政策环境和积极的财政政策大概率都将延续。我们认为
2027 年可能需要关注供给端的持续稳健经营：一方面，美国对 AI 科技发
展的监管政策前景存在不确定性，对于当前全球范围内 AI 带动的经济增
长结构或产生一定影响，进而影响我国外需，另一方面，全球范围内能
源价格上涨、国内 PPI 同比高增和人民币的升值趋势，都可能推升供给端
成本，进而影响企业生产经营。我们认为不排除 2027 年经济增长目标设
定为 2026 年增长目标区间的下限。 
◼ 风险提示：全球通胀二次上行；欧美经济超预期回落；国际局势复杂化。

### Page 2

2026 年 11 月 16 日 10 月经济数据点评 2

工业增加值同比增速回落 
10 月工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，较 9 月下降 0.7 个百分点，较去年同期下降 0.1 个百分点，低于万
得一致预期同比增速 5.32%。

图表 1. 工业增加值同比增速

资料来源：万得，中银证券

从环比增速来看，10 月工业增加值环比上升 0.21%，与 2016 年-2019 年期间同期平均水平 0.47%相
比偏低,略低于去年同期的环比增速 0.22%，10 月工业增加值环比较 9 月下降,趋势上符合季节性规律，
但受 9 月环比基数偏高影响，10 月环比增速回落幅度较大。 
从行业分类来看，1-10 月采矿业工业增加值累计同比增速 1.9%，制造业累计同比增速 5.7%，公用
事业累计同比增速 5.3%，高技术产业累计同比增速 14.6%。从企业类型来看，1-10 月国企工业增加
值同比增速 3.6%，股份制企业同比增速 5.7%，外企工业增加值同比增速 3.2%，私企工业增加值同
比增速 4.2%。与 1-9 月相比，1-10 月累计同比增速上升的仅有高技术产业,下降的是国企、采矿业、
公共事业、股份制企业和私企。10 月当月工业增加值同比增速相较 9 月，各行业和企业类型中工业
增加值同比增速上升的也仅有高技术产业，下降幅度超过 1.0 个百分点的是国企和公共事业。 
1-10 月，装备制造业增加值同比增长 10.6%，高技术制造业增加值增长 17.6%，分别快于全部规模
以上工业增加值 5.8 和 12.8 个百分点；分产品看，3D 打印设备、锂离子电池、工业机器人产品产量
同比分别增长 85.9%、30.0%、26.0%。

图表 2. 工业增加值环比增速  图表 3. 主要行业工业增加值当月同比增速

### Page 3

2026 年 11 月 16 日 10 月经济数据点评 3

10 月，采矿业和大宗周期品加工业当月工业增加值同比增速偏弱，中游设备制造业工业增加值表现
相对较好。 
采矿业方面，1-10 月煤炭开采和洗选业增加值累计同比下降 0.1%，石油和天然气开采业增加值累计
同比增长 6.1%；从当月同比增速来看，较 9 月增速小幅上升的是煤炭开采，小幅下降的是油气开采，
但煤炭开采行业 10 月当月工业增加值同比仍在负增长区间。 
消费类制造业方面，1-10 月农副食品加工业增加值累计同比增长 4.5%，纺织业增加值累计同比增长
3.0%，医药制造业增加值累计同比增长 5.3%；从当月同比增速来看，较 9 月增速上升的是医药制造
业，降幅超过 1.0 个百分点的是纺织业。 
大宗周期品加工业方面，1-10 月化学原料及化学制品制造业增加值累计同比增长 3.3%，非金属矿物
制品业增加值累计同比下降 2.9%，黑色金属冶炼及压延加工业增加值累计同比增长 1.4%，有色金属
冶炼及压延加工业增加值累计同比下降 1.9%；从当月同比增速来看，较 9 月增速上升的有色金属和
非金属矿物，增速升幅较小，但较 9 月增速下降的化学原料和黑色金属降幅均超过 1.0 个百分点。 
中游设备制造业方面，1-10 月通用设备制造业增加值累计同比增长 8.2%，专用设备制造业增加值累
计同比增长 9.7%，汽车制造业增加值累计同比增长 6.5%，电气机械及器材制造业增加值累计同比增
长 7.2%，计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业增加值累计同比增长 16.2%；从当月同比增速来看，
较 9 月增速上升的仅有计算机通信，且升幅达到 3.0 个百分点，下降的是汽车制造业和专用设备制
造业，其中汽车制造业较 9 月降幅达到 5.8 个百分点，10 月当月工业增加值同比增速仅为 0.9%。

### Page 4

2026 年 11 月 16 日 10 月经济数据点评 4

10 月外需延续较强走势，出口交货值同比继续大幅上升，产销率较去年同期小幅上升。 
10 月规模以上工业企业产销率为 96.5%，较 9 月下降 0.5 个百分点，较去年同期上升 0.1 个百分点，
当月同比增速下降 0.4%；1-10 月规模以上工业企业产销率为 96.4%，较去年同期上升 0.4 个百分点，
累计同比增速持平。 
10 月规模以上工业企业出口交货值同比增长 18.6%，较 9 月上升 1.6 个百分点，1-10 月规模以上工
业企业出口交货值累计同比增长 11.6%，较 1-9 月上升 0.9 个百分点。

图表 8. 产销率当月值和累计同比增速  图表 9. 出口交货值同比增速

资料来源：万得，中银证券  资料来源：万得，中银证券

社零增速再度回落 
10 月社零同比增长 0.4%，较 9 月下降0.7 个百分点，其中，除汽车以外的消费品零售额同比增长2.5%，
较 9 月下降 1.4 个百分点；1-10 月社零累计同比增长 1.0%，其中，服务消费累计同比增长 4.9%，较
1-9 月下降 0.1 个百分点。 
10 月社零环比增速增长 0.02%，较去年同期环比增速下降 0.09 个百分点，较历史同期平均水平明显
偏低。

图表 10. 社会消费品零售额同比增速

### Page 5

2026 年 11 月 16 日 10 月经济数据点评 5

图表 11. 汽车以外社零和服务消费增速  图表 12. 社零环比增速的历史波动

资料来源：万得，中银证券  资料来源：万得，中银证券

1-10 月网上商品和服务零售额累计同比增长 3.7%，增速较 1-9 月下降 0.7 个百分点，其中实物商品
网上零售额累计同比增长 2.9%，增速较 1-9 月下降 0.9 个百分点。10 月网上零售额累计占比为 40.8%，
较 2026 年同期上升 9.7 个百分点，较 1-9 月下降 0.2 个百分点。 
10 月商品消费同比增长 0.3%，较 9 月下降 0.7 个百分点，限额以上商品消费同比下降 5.1%，较 9
月下降 2.6 个百分点；10 月餐饮消费同比增长 0.7%，较 9 月下降 1.4 个百分点，限额以上餐饮消费
同比下降 2.9%，较 9 月下降 4.9 个百分点。1-10 月，在服务零售额中，通讯信息服务类、旅游咨询
租赁服务类、文体休闲服务类零售额较快增长。 
1-10 月限额以上企业消费品零售总额累计同比下降 1.9%，限额以上企业商品消费累计同比下降 2.1%,
弱于餐饮消费累计同比增速 0.9%。

图表 13. 网上零售总额的占比和增速  图表 14. 限额以上企业社零增速

资料来源：万得，中银证券  资料来源：万得，中银证券

10 月受价格因素影响，石油制品消费增速有所上升，房地产后周期消费依然偏弱，国庆假期时间较
长，一定程度上拖累了居民消费增速表现。 
从产品分类来看，10 月限额以上企业商品零售总额增速较高的细分行业包括通讯器材类（6.5%）、
石油及制品类（4.4%）和文化办公用品类（4.1%），增速较低的细分行业包括金银珠宝类（-28.7%）、
汽车类（-21.4%）和建筑及装潢材料类（-11.2%）；与 9 月相比，增速上升的有石油及制品类、体
育娱乐用品类、家用电器和音像器材类和家具，增速下降较多的有通讯器材类、金银珠宝类和烟酒
类等。

2026 年 11 月 16 日 10 月经济数据点评 7

图表 18. 固定资产投资环比增速的季节规律  图表 19. 固投各项构成分类的累计同比增速

资料来源：万得，中银证券  资料来源：万得，中银证券

10 月制造业、基建和房地产当月投资增速较 9 月均出现回落。 
从产业分类来看，第一产业累计同比下降 6.1%，第二产业累计同比下降 3.6%，第三产业累计同比下
降 10.9%；从地区来看，东部累计同比下降 11.5%，中部下降 12.9%，西部下降 9.2%，东北下降 25.9%；
从企业类型来看，内资企业累计同比下降 7.9%，国有控股同比下降 4.0%，外商投资下降 6.6%。 
分主要行业看，制造业投资 1-10 月累计同比下降 2.9%，基建投资累计同比下降 7.3%，地产投资累
计同比下降 21.6%。从我们测算的当月同比增速看，10 月制造业投资同比增速下降 9.3%，较 9 月下
降 8.4 个百分点，基建投资同比增速下降 17.4%，较 9 月下降 6.9 个百分点，房地产投资同比增速下
降 34.2%，较 9 月下降 7.0 个百分点。 
1-10 月固投增速在正增长区间的一级分类行业是采矿业（2.5%）和交运仓储（0.7%），固投增速较
低的行业包括文体娱（-18.5%）、教育（-15.7%）和水利环境（-14.4%）。制造业行业分类下，固
投增速较高的细分行业包括铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设备制造业（14.1%）、计算机、通信
和其他电子设备制造业（9.9%）和纺织业（2.8%），固投增速较低的行业包括食品制造业（-10.7%）、
金属制品业（-9.9%）和医药制造业（-9.2%）。 
与 1-9 月相比，一级分类行业中固投增速降幅较小的行业是卫生社会、教育和采矿业，固投增速下
降较多的行业是农林牧渔业、水利环境和文体娱。制造业行业分类下，固投增速上行的行业仅有计
算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业、有色金属冶炼及压延加工业和金属制品业，固投增速下行较多
的行业是通用设备制造业、铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设备制造业和汽车制造业。 
据统计局解读，1-10 月知识产权产品投资同比增长 9.2%，高技术产业投资同比增长 4.0%，其中信
息服务业，航空、航天器及设备制造业，电子及通信设备制造业投资分别增长 20.5%、10.2%、6.6%。

### Page 10

2026 年 11 月 16 日 10 月经济数据点评 10

结论：以稳定的生产端应对需求端波动 
10 月经济数据偏弱。工业增加值同比增速较 9 月下行 0.7 个百分点，且低于万得一致预期，社零同
比增速较 9 月下行 0.7 个百分点，且低于万得一致预期，1-10 月固定资产投资累计同比增速较 1-9
月下行 0.9 个百分点，且低于万得一致预期。从细项看，10 月经济数据中表现较好的是工业增加值
中的高技术产业，以及依托外需的出口交货值，此前经济数据中表现较好的部分，如固定资产投资
中的设备购置累计同比增速、社零中的通信工具当月同比增速，在 10 月也都出现了增速下降的情况。
另一方面，此前偏弱的数据尚未出现好转迹象，包括房地产固定资产投资增速和商品房销售数据同
比增速仍在下降，基建投资增速仍在下滑等等。考虑到今年的国庆和中秋假期相邻，部分居民选择
利用年假增加国庆前后出行时间，我们认为 10 月经济数据偏弱，一定程度上可能有假期安排对工作
日天数缩短的影响。 
关注 2027 年经济增长目标设定。12 月政治局会议时间一般在上旬，这就意味着 10 月经济数据对于
定调 2027 年经济工作的影响较大。从 10 月数据的特点来看，我们认为较为确定的几个方向：一是
三驾马车中净出口仍将是 2027 年经济增长的重要拉动力，二是消费偏弱的状态大概率将延续，三是
基建投资增速仍较大程度上依赖财政支出，短期内的亮点可能是“六张网”，四是房地产行业即便有
政策支持，整体或仍在寻底的过程中，连带房地产产业链上下游中期内都将处于寻底过程，五是高
技术产业或将保持较好增速，六是国内通胀压力整体处于偏低水平，七是适度宽松的货币政策环境
和积极的财政政策大概率都将延续。我们认为 2027 年可能需要关注供给端的持续稳健经营：一方面，
美国对 AI 科技发展的监管政策前景存在不确定性，对于当前全球范围内 AI 带动的经济增长结构或
产生一定影响，进而影响我国外需，另一方面，全球范围内能源价格上涨、国内 PPI 同比高增和人
民币的升值趋势，都可能推升供给端成本，进而影响企业生产经营。我们认为不排除 2027 年经济增
长目标设定为 2026 年增长目标区间的下限。 
风险提示：全球通胀二次上行；欧美经济超预期回落；国际局势复杂化。

2026 年 11 月 16 日 10 月经济数据点评 11

披露声明 
本报告准确表述了证券分析师的个人观点。该证券分析师声明，本人未在公司内、外部机构兼任有损本人独立性与客
观性的其他职务，没有担任本报告评论的上市公司的董事、监事或高级管理人员；也不拥有与该上市公司有关的任何
财务权益；本报告评论的上市公司或其它第三方都没有或没有承诺向本人提供与本报告有关的任何补偿或其它利益。 
中银国际证券股份有限公司同时声明，将通过公司网站披露本公司授权公众媒体及其他机构刊载或者转发证券研究报
告有关情况。如有投资者于未经授权的公众媒体看到或从其他机构获得本研究报告的，请慎重使用所获得的研究报告，
以防止被误导，中银国际证券股份有限公司不对其报告理解和使用承担任何责任。 
评级体系说明 
以报告发布日后公司股价/行业指数涨跌幅相对同期相关市场指数的涨跌幅的表现为基准： 
公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

沪深市场基准指数为沪深 300 指数；新三板市场基准指数为三板成指或三板做市指数；香港市场基准指数为恒生指数
或恒生中国企业指数；美股市场基准指数为纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数。

10 月金融数据点评：如何看社融增速回升？

[Table_Author] 
 沈夏宜 分析师  魏争 分析师

Email:shenxiayi@lczq.com Email:weizheng@lczq.com  
证书:S1320523020004 证书:S1320524100001

### Page 2

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 2 
增 6983 亿元，是当月社融的主要支撑。一是去年同期到期规模较高，形
成较低基数。今年 3 月以来，月均国债发行稳定在 1.3 万亿左右，国债净
融资规模与当月到期规模关系较大；二是专项债发行仍保持一定强度，当
月新增专项债 3280 亿元，同比多增 406 亿元。 
企业债券、委托贷款、外币贷款均同比多增。当月新增企业债券 3446 亿
元，同比多增 946 亿元。当月债券利率下行，推动更多企业“以债替贷”
的融资转变；当月新增委托贷款 1862 亿元，同比多增 208 亿元。新型政
策性金融工具在 10 月全部投放完毕，集中投放对委托贷款形成支撑；当
月新增外币贷款 95 亿，同比多增 295 亿。10 月国内进口同比增长 
41.6%，进口付汇需求较强。此外，人民币汇率偏强亦对外币贷款需求形
成一定支撑。 
M1 增速回升。当月 M1 同比增长 4.4%，较上月回升 1.2 个百分点。一是
去年同期基数偏低；二是新型政策性金融工具集中投放，以及 PSL 利率
下调、支持扩围，对企业活期资金形成一定支撑；三是当月人民币延续升
值趋势，持续推动企业结汇意愿。 
M2 增速小幅回落。10 月 M2 同比增长 7.1%，较上月回落 0.1 个百分
点。一是当月社融主要增量来自于政府部门，但当月财政存款同比多增
6800 亿，向企业和居民部门释放偏慢；二是信贷增速续降，存款派生效
应偏弱。 
社融增速有望保持平稳。往前看，一是货币端，10 月末央行优化公开市
场操作机制，将隔夜逆回购转为日常操作，强化短端利率调控，有助于稳
定银行体系流动性；二是需求端，10 月 19 日，发改委等部门出台消费基
础设施建设指导意见，支持商业、文旅、交通、养老等领域设施建设，有
望拓宽相关领域融资需求；三是财政端，地方政府债务结存限额发行使用
进入年末收官阶段，叠加前期政策资金逐步形成实物工作量，项目配套融
资有望跟进，带动信贷融资边际修复。

风险提示：宏观经济不及预期，地产销售不及预期，美国货币政策超预
期，地缘政治风险超预期。

投资评级说明 
投资建议的评级标准  评级 说明 
评级标准为报告发布日后的 6 个月内公司股价（或行业
指数）相对同期基准指数的相对市场表现。其中 A 股市
场以沪深 300 指数为基准；新三板市场以三板成指（针
对协议转让标的）或三板做市指数（针对做市转让标的）
为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准。 
股票评级 
买入 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 10%以上 
增持 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 5%~10%之间 
中性 相对同期基准指数涨幅在-5%~+5%之间 
减持 相对同期基准指数跌幅在 5%以上 
行业评级 
看好 相对表现优于市场 
中性 相对表现与市场持平 
看淡 相对表现弱于市场

### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國聯準會如市場預期，維持政策利率不變。決策官員仍對通膨保持擔憂，也承
認美國經濟正以「強勁速度」成長。聯準會主席 Kevin Warsh 在會後談話中，並
未明確排除進一步緊縮政策的可能，激勵市場對 12 月升息的預期升溫。數據方
面，美國第三季 GDP 年化成長率達 3.4%，高於前值的 2.2%，亦優於市場預期
的 3.2%，印證美國經濟動能強勁。此外，9 月 PCE 與核心 PCE 符合預期，但凸
顯通膨壓力持續偏高。在「通膨高企」與「經濟強韌」雙重因素驅動下，市場預
期聯準會將於 12 月升息，為美元指數提供有力支撐。惟近期受 AI 產業股票波
動加劇影響，部分資金轉入美債避險，導致美元指數在高檔面臨調節性賣壓。
⚫ 儘管受能源價格走揚影響，9 月歐元區通膨率升至 3.8%，但在薪資壓力維持溫
和，且歐洲央行尚未觀察到第二輪通膨效應全面擴散的情況下，歐洲央行上週決
議維持政策利率不變。通膨回升令市場預期歐洲央行將在下次會議升息，為歐元

提供部分支撐，但考量歐元區經濟成長表現仍落後美國，在美元基本面強勁的抑
制下，歐元反彈力道相對有限。目前歐元最大的潛在風險在於部分國家的債券市
場壓力尚未消散，市場對歐洲財政風險仍保持高度警戒。
⚫ 日本央行維持政策利率於 1.25%不變，並且首次明確表示已達成其 2%的通膨目
標；過去日本央行雖認為通膨改善，但始終強調尚未穩定達標，如今首次正式承
認達成 2%目標，代表未來升息門檻進一步下降。此外，日本央行將經濟成長預
測維持在 0.7%至 0.8%，並小幅上修 CPI 預測，令市場預期日央 12 月有望再次
升息，日本貨幣政策正常化將有助日圓結束疲軟走勢。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Nov/2/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週台幣匯價高點為 32.390，低點在 32.676，以 32.478 作收，較前一周收盤價
升值 3 分。 
上週台幣先升後貶，周初，市場等待聯準會利率決議，交投偏謹慎觀望，而外
資在前周大幅匯出之後，轉為雙向進出，台幣止跌回穩，最高來到 32.390 的周內高
點；然而周間，美國科技股財報表現失色，台股賣壓沉重，外資賣超台股並再度轉
為大幅匯出，台幣走貶，最低來到 32.676，不過匯價逢高吸引出口商賣壓出籠，加
上央行提供流動性，台幣貶幅收斂，終場在央行調節之下收在 32.478。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣偏弱震盪。就國際市場來看，聯準會 10 月利率決策維持利率不
變，但有兩名官員主張升息 1 碼，且聯準會表示通膨仍處於高檔，顯示聯準會對通
膨仍保持警戒，市場預期美國利率將維持高檔，台美利差仍維持在相對高位，不利
台幣表現；另一方面，美國科技股財報表現不如預期，引發市場對 AI 投資回報的疑
慮，對台股也帶來壓力。就台灣市場來看，外資持續調節台股並大幅匯出，10 月累
計賣超將近兆元，資金匯出壓力大，不過一方面央行持續進場調節，加上匯價逢高
預計吸引出口商進場拋匯，料將限制台幣貶值速度。綜合以上，預估本週台幣走勢
偏弱。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.300-32.800

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Nov/2/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣持續走升。周初市場等待美國聯準會利率決議，交投偏向觀望，境
外人民幣主要於 6.6600 上方震盪；周間聯準會如預期維持利率不變，美元指數自 102
上方回落，加上中國出口結匯需求持續提供支撐，境外人民幣突破 6.6600 整數關口；
週五人行中間價續創近三年九個月新高，最高來到 6.7138，顯示中國人行允許人民
幣溫和升值的走勢，境外人民幣一度升破 6.6500 整數關口，最高來到 6.6487，終場
收在 6.6528，週線升值 162 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6558，週線升值 146 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣偏升。就中國國內市場來看，10 月官方製造業 PMI 降至 49.3，
重新落入景氣收縮區間，反映中國內需及製造業動能仍有待改善，不過 9 月出口年
增 36.3%，出口商結匯需求持續提供人民幣支撐，加上人行中間價續創近三年九個
月新高，預計將持續帶動人民幣走升；就國際市場來看，聯準會 10 月利率決策維持
利率不變，但有兩名官員主張升息 1 碼，顯示聯準會對通膨仍保持警戒，市場預期
美國利率將維持高檔，美中利差維持在相對高位，將限制人民幣升值的速度與幅度；
最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，各天期均線向下發散，匯價維持在五日均
線之下交投，均線呈現空頭排列，顯示空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣
偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6200-6.6700

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Nov/02/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 上漲。週初，美伊衝突仍陷僵局支撐美元避險買盤，USD/JPY 最
高來到 158.34；週間，聯準會公布利率維持利率不變，USD/JPY 持續在 158 附近盤
整；臨近週末，日本央行公布利率維持不變並釋放偏鷹訊號，USD/JPY 最低來到
156.94，終場收在 157.53，週線上漲 72 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本央行於上周五宣布利
率維持在 1.25%不變，並表示隨著基礎通膨率大致達到 2%，央行必須持續控制通
膨穩定於此目標，且再次強調貨幣政策已進入更為緊縮階段，未來每隔數月即可能
升息。此外，日本央行在季度報告中，亦將截至 2028 年 3 月財年的核心消費者通
膨預估自三個月前預測的 2.4%上調至 2.6%。日本央行偏鷹立場令市場預期日本最
快將於 12 月再次升息，預計將有利日圓表現。二、聯準會宣布維持利率於 3.75%-
4%區間不變，惟因通膨仍處高位，聯準會重申將致力實現 2%的長期通膨區間， 
FedWatch 顯示 12 月升息機率約七成，美元持穩，預計將限縮 USD/JPY 下行空
間。三、就技術面而言，在日線圖中，USD/JPY 各天期均線趨平，近期匯價大致
在 156 至 158 間震盪，匯價暫無明確方向性。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現
區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日圓：156.00-158.50 
前週交易區間 
156.63 158.34

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Nov/02/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，美伊衝突持續以及法國政經情勢動盪等情況下，
歐元走跌，最低來到 1.1111；週間，聯準會和歐洲央行相繼公布利率決議均維持不
變，歐洲央行發表言論偏鷹派，歐元止跌回升至 1.1214；臨近週末，歐元區公布經
濟數據好壞參半，歐元自高點回落，終場收在 1.1158，週線下跌 41 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、歐洲央行如市場預期宣布
存款利率維持在 2.5%不變，並警告能源成本持續上漲將對通膨造成上行風險，暗示
可能在 12 月再次升息，升息預期升溫預計將支撐歐元走勢。二、歐元區第三季 GDP
年增 1.3%，高於市場預期的 1.1%，顯示在能源價格持續上漲的不利因素下，經濟仍
展現韌性，預計有利歐元表現。三、聯準會宣布維持利率在 3.75%-4%不變，並重申
致力控制通膨的立場。此外，美國公布第三季 GDP 成長 3.4%，優於市場預期的 3.2%，
9 月核心 PCE 年增率自前月的 3%上升至 3.1%，在美國經濟強勁及通膨仍高的情況
下，市場預期聯準會將於 12 月升息一碼，美元持穩，預計將限縮歐元走升空間。四、
技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 各天期均線向下發散，不過近期匯價在多次向
下測試 1.1120 附近均未有效跌破，顯示下方仍有買盤支撐。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD
本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1050-1.1270 
前週交易區間 
1.1103 1.1226

### Page 7

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.10.27-115.10.30 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.66% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15109R 2.03% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.845%~1.846%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，時序邁入月底，銀行間已大致調整好積數，且央行未明顯
增發定存單，令市場資金充裕。近兩日股市回落，令週五資金需求上揚，惟供
需尚屬平衡，因此短率持穩於近期區間。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.825% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.66%~1.68%，利率大致持平於上週。 
二、 本週台債市場交投依舊清淡，10 年期指標券 A15109R 等殖系統成交在 2.03%，
5 年期指標券 A15108 議價成交在 1.845%~1.846%。 
美債利率自波段高點回落，且央行總裁楊金龍本週立法院發言維持近來對通
膨容忍度變寬之態度，惟市場對央行升息預期並未完全消除，買盤追價動力有
限，預期台債殖利率維持區間整理，關注下週 30 年期公債標售果，預估 5 年
券區間為 1.80%~1.85%，10 年券區間為 1.99%~2.04%。

2 群益投顧 
  ESG：   
環境：鴻海採用 TCFD 框架為基礎，全面盤點與評估氣候相關風險與機會，
並導入 ISO 14001 環境管理系統與 ISO 45001 職業安全衛生管理系統，同時
也推動「低碳供應鏈轉型推動計畫」，協助超過 30 家供應商完成節能專案，
累計減少碳排放約 15,000 公噸 CO₂e。鴻海已有超過 150 家供應商完成碳盤
查並簽署科學基礎減碳目標（SBTi），提升能源使用效率與減碳成效。  
社會：鴻海秉持「以人為本」理念，長期支持教育與青年培訓，設立「三創
育成基金會」，提供新創團隊資金與資源支持，自 2015 年以來已經培育超
過  100 組科技創業團隊，促進創新創業生態系發展，為社會培訓科技人才。
治理：董事會有設立「永續發展委員會」，主管由具備三年以上公開發行公
司財務主管經歷之財務長兼任，該委員自 2023 年起每年召開至少四次會議，
全體委員及相關主管亦於任職期間完成 ESG 專業訓練課程，強化永續治理
能力與資訊揭露透明度。 
公司簡介：  
鴻海精密工業股份有限公司（Hon Hai Precision Industry），成立於 1974 年，
由郭台銘創辦，總部位於新北市土城區。公司早期以模具及電子零組件製造
起家，逐步擴展至電子產品組裝、系統整合及設計製造服務，現為全球最大
電子專業製造服務（EMS）廠商。 
集團業務涵蓋消費智能產品、雲端網路產品、電腦終端產品及元件與其他產
品四大領域，旗下包含工業富聯（FII）、鴻騰精密（FIT）等重要子公司，
並透過全球事業單位及子公司分工，提供零組件、模組至整機系統的垂直整
合服務。集團生產及服務據點遍及全球逾 20 個國家及地區，包括中國、台
灣、印度、越南、墨西哥及美國等，全球員工人數於季節性高峰約達 100 萬
人。 
鴻海主要競爭對手包括廣達、緯創、和碩、仁寶及立訊精密等，依產品領域
有所不同。近年積極布局 AI 伺服器、電動車及半導體等新興業務，其中 AI
伺服器受惠全球雲端服務供應商（CSP）資本支出擴張，成為重要成長動能，
並持續鞏固全球電子製造服務龍頭地位。 
3Q26 營收 3.02 兆元，稅後 EPS 5.51 元： 3Q26 營收 3.02 兆元，QoQ+19%，
YoY+46%，毛利率 6.02%，QoQ-0.1ppts，YoY-0.33ppts，主要受產品組合及
原物料上漲影響導致毛利率下滑；營業利益 1,154.99 億元，QoQ+22%，
YoY+64%，營益率 3.83%， QoQ+0.07ppts， YoY+0.4ppts，營業利益成長高
於營收及營業毛利，顯現隨營運規模擴大，生產進入規模經濟；歸屬母公司
淨利 775.24 億元，QoQ+29%， YoY+34%，稅後 EPS 5.51 元。營業利益、淨
利和 EPS 皆創下歷史單季新高。 
10/2026 營收 MoM+12.7%，預估 4Q26 營收 QoQ+11.7%：鴻海 10/2026 營
收 1.31 兆元，MoM+12.7%， YoY+45.7%，創單月歷史新高，除受惠 GB300
持續放量外，iPhone 新機推出亦帶動營收顯著增長；Vera Rubin 僅小量出
貨。展望 4Q26，我們預期 GB200/300 出貨量季增持平至微幅下滑，Vera 
Rubin 則有雙位數以上的成長，並且由於 Vera Rubin 的 ASP 近乎倍增，預
估伺服器營收將季增雙位數。消費電子方面，我們認為記憶體與處理器成本

3 群益投顧 
上升，將使手機出貨面臨壓力，但產品 ASP 提升及 Apple 穩健的品牌效應
仍將為出貨量提供支撐，因此 4Q26 仍將受惠新機推出與傳統旺季帶來的成
長。相較於消費電子的季節性，GB200/300 放量與產品組合變化對全年營運
的影響更大，因此 2026 年的主要成長引擎仍是伺服器業務的擴張；Vera 
Rubin 則為 2027 年的成長建立基礎。綜上所述，群益預估 4Q26 營收 3.37
兆元，QoQ+11.7%；毛利率/營益率持續受 Vera Rubin 新平台出貨而稀釋，
下滑 0.10ppts/0.06ppts 來到 5.92%/3.77%，歸屬母公司淨利 899.02 億元，
QoQ+15.97%，稅後 EPS 6.39 元。 
雲端網路成為營運主軸，規模經濟支持本業獲利成長： 3Q26 雲端網路產品
占營收 54%，高於 2Q26 的 51%。消費智能、電腦終端及元件與其他產品分
別占 29%、12%及 5%。雲端網路產品受 AI 伺服器及機櫃出貨帶動，季增與
年增均強勁。消費智能產品亦受新品與傳統旺季帶動，季增優於公司原先預
期。據我們所知，伺服器約占雲端網路營收的 85%-90%，其中約 70%來自
AI 伺服器。隨雲端網路占比提高，AI 機櫃的交付時程、平台組合及物料認
列方式，將更直接影響鴻海的營收與獲利率。鴻海 3Q26 毛利率 6.02%，QoQ-
0.1ppts，主要受到整櫃 GPU 伺服器高昂的 BOM 所 稀 釋；營 業 利 益 率 3.83%，
QoQ+0.07ppts，雖然毛利率季減，營益率卻有所改善，反映出營收規模擴大
及營運效率提升的效益。 
Vera Rubin 接力 GB300，2027 年進入新平台主要貢獻期：AI 機櫃的新舊平
台交替是鴻海延續成長的核心動能。鴻海預計 2026 年 AI 伺服器機櫃出貨
較 2025 年倍增以上，且既有 GB 平台需求可能延續至 2027 年。此外，Vera 
Rubin 與 GB 系列同為 NVL72，因此我們預期鴻海將可較平滑的實現世代交
替。管理層預估 4Q26 Vera Rubin出貨量將呈高雙位數增長。展望 2027 年，
伺服器業務上會新增更多 Neocloud 客戶，但主要客戶仍然是大型 CSP。整
體訂單並未隨近期市場的擔憂而調整，對於 AI 投資的展望維持正向，預估
AI 機櫃出貨量將呈高雙位數成長。 
自製零組件與自動化支撐獲利，高 BOM 仍主導毛利率走勢：鴻海透過垂直
整合提高單一專案可承接的製造內容，管理層表示除規模經濟外，零組件自
製率的提高與自動化生產都將為毛利率及營益率提供支撐。根據我們調查，
在晶片以外的零組件自製率約 50%，布局包括連接器、線材、Midplane、交
換器及散熱相關產品，零組件毛利率高於 15%，部分甚至可達 20%，管理層
亦提出 800G 以上交換器全年營收倍增的展望，這些產品有助鴻海由機櫃組
裝向高速互聯及關鍵元件延伸，並可提升附加價值。然而據我們了解目前鴻
海在 GPU 的大型專案中尚未採用 consignment model，記憶體等元件漲價則
主要透過 cost-plus 模式轉嫁給客戶，進入 Vera Rubin 世代後，由於 ASP 將
近倍增，會進一步造成毛利率稀釋，考慮到 GPU Rack 單價極高，我們預估
2027 年毛利率仍將承壓。 
ASIC 拓展產品組合，物料模式與 ASP 差異使營收比重低於出貨量佔比：據
我們了解，鴻海 ASIC 平台約佔 AI 伺服器營收的 20%，較 1H26 略升，且
ASIC 平台成長速度高於 GPU 平台，除既有客戶外亦有新專案，管理層預估
3Q26 ASIC 伺服器出貨量 YoY+150%，高於 GPU 伺服器 70%的出貨量年增。
我們認為 ASIC 專案增加可擴大鴻海在不同運算平台的參與，減少成長來源
集中於單一 GPU 平台的程度。此外，大部分 ASIC 專案將有望採用
consignment model，有助於緩解 AI 伺服器對於整體毛利率的負面效果，然
而 GPU 平台 ASP 顯著高於 ASIC 平台，因此 ASIC 的營收較難完整反映其

4 群益投顧 
專案及製造規模。我們認為目前 ASIC 專案採用 consignment model 尚不足
以顯著改善 GPU Rack 高 BOM 對整體毛利率的稀釋。 
策略投資三菱電機，電動車可望成為未來另一成長引擎：鴻海透過與三菱電
機的策略結盟深化在電動車領域的發展，雖投資金額小於 AI 相關的支出，
但將持續投入研發，目前已做好充足準備量產，將尋機大規模進入 EV 市場，
可望成為未來的另一成長動能。 
預估 2027 年營收 15.1 兆元，稅後 EPS 24.71 元：我們預估隨 AI 機櫃放量
將提高營收與獲利規模，2027 年營收 15.1 兆元，YoY+37.1%，毛利率 5.65%，
YoY-0.4ppts；營業利益 5,181.73 億元，YoY+25.5%，營益率 3.43%，YoY-
0.3ppts；歸屬母公司淨利 3,478.27 億元，YoY+25.4%，稅後 EPS 24.71 元。
Vera Rubin 由初期出貨轉為全年營收貢獻，加上既有 GB300 需求延續及
ASIC 業務擴張，帶動全年營收顯著增長。公司持續布局台灣、美國、墨西
哥及越南產能，亦有助因應客戶在不同地區的交付需求。 
投資建議：受惠 AI 伺服器的強勁需求，2026 AI 機櫃出貨可望倍數增長。
此外我們預期憑藉 iPhone 的品牌效應，4Q26 智慧型消費電子業務可望維持
過往的傳統出貨旺季。展望 2027 年，Vera Rubin 將成為主要成長引擎，主
要客戶對於 AI 伺服器的需求依舊強勁。關鍵製程自動化及零組件自製率的
提升亦有助提升營運效率，為毛利率及營益率提供部分支撐。考量上述原
因，我們給予鴻海 Trading Buy 的投資建議，目標價 272 元。

8 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

### Page 1

All Ring

Accelerated advanced packaging and CPO 
ramp optionality at attractive valuation

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 1,750 TWD | Price: 1,025 TWD

2H26 strength sustains on 2.5D packaging expansion 
3Q26 sales were +41% QoQ above Street estimates on solid CoWoS and FOCoS capacity 
expansion across its foundry and OSAT customers, backed by the accelerated capex from 
back-end industry to accommodate stronger cloud AI chipset demand outlook into ‘27. 
With mix shifted toward margin-diluted automation tools, 3Q26 GMs were -630 bps QoQ 
to 48.5% (vs. BofAe 54.1%) though OpMs held resilient QoQ at 32.9% on operating 
leverage, taking 3Q26 EPS to NT$8.50 (vs. BofAe/Street NT$10.74/NT$9.34.). For 4Q26, 
we estimate sales -9% QoQ due to CoWoS revenue recognition timing partially offset by 
sustained strength in FOCoS. We expect GMs in 4Q26 to recover to its long-term guidance 
of 50-55% range (BofAe 53.7%) on better product mix.   
Diverse 2.5D/3D packaging and CPO ramp lifting ‘27-28 
All Ring’s sales are on track to +88% YoY in ‘26 and could see continued strength into ‘27 
(BofAe +55% YoY), mainly led by 80-90k WPM CoWoS addition from TSMC to 200-210k 
WPM by 4Q27 along with ASE’s accelerated FOCoS capacity expansion and CoWoS full 
process ramp for outsourced business. With superior throughput and yield stability vs. its 
peers, we expect its CPO optical coupling equipment shipment to reach 150 units in ‘27, 
backed by a solid commitment from Nvidia/SPIL to increase CPO assembly capacity by 15x 
(see our report: Implication from Nvidia GTC: back-end capacity constrained; CPO 
accelerates). Broader equipment share and content gains across diversifying advanced 
packaging technologies, including FOPLP, SoIC, CoPoS and US back-end solutions, 
alongside the ramp-up of CPO equipment should drive All Ring’s advanced packaging 
revenue +60% YoY in 2027, lifting GM/OpM to 54.2%/33.3% from 52%/32.2% in 2026.  
Reiterate Buy on decent EPS upcycle with +34% CAGR 
We maintain our 2027/28 EPS unchanged at NT$49.34/54.67, our Buy rating on All Ring 
and PO of NT$1,750 still based on 36x 2027E P/E (vs. its 8-41x range). We believe 
decent sales/earnings CAGR of +30%/+34% over '26-'28E supported by solid advanced 
packaging capacity build and smooth CPO ramp should warrant high valuation.

Company Sector 
Semiconductor Capital Equipment

Company Description 
Founded in 1996, All Ring is a Taiwan-based semiconductor 
equipment supplier with core technologies spanning 
dispensing, attachment, AOI, optical coupling, and precision 
automation. Building on its initial expertise in passive 
component manufacturing equipment, the company has 
gradually expanded into the back-end semiconductor 
packaging market and established long-term partnerships 
with leading foundry and OSATs customers.

Investment Rationale 
We have a Buy rating on All Ring, in view of its solid market 
position in the semiconductor advanced packaging 
equipment. We believe its long-standing relationships with 
leading foundry and OSATs, coupled with strong technology 
development capabilities, position the company well to 
capture the secular uptrend in AI-driven advanced 
packaging capacity expansion and rising equipment 
content.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
All Ring (XNQNF) 
Our PO of NT$1,750 is based on 36x 2027E P/E, positioned at the upper half of its 
historical trading range of 8-41x. We believe P/E based valuation is appropriate given All 
Ring's robust top-line visibility, stable profitability profile, and structural earnings 
improvement on decent exposure to AI-driven advanced packaging upcycle. The 
valuation multiple is underpinned by 
1) 34%/37% CAGR in sales and earnings over 2026-28E and 2) sustained ROE in the 30-
40% range

Downside risks are: 
1) slowdown in advanced packaging capacity expansion and 2) mis-execution on 
equipment development

Update
AllRing Tech Co.  | Asia Pacific
3Q26   margin miss from product
mix
3Q26 earnings missed our estimate by 13% and consensus by 16%, given less
favorable product mix...   There was less  inspection (gross margin >60%) but more
automation tools (gross margin ~40%).
...as well as weaker subsidiary company performance.
Looking into 4Q26, we expect gross margin to recover to >50% from improving
product mix: However, this will still be slightly offset by new product shipped to

the OSATs, which carries lower margin in the early ramping phase.
We think CoWoS should still account for 70-80% of 2027e total revenue – with
other new customers and tools, including a US customer, CPO, and CoW process.
We think CPO could account for 15-20% of 2027e revenue, with shipments up >10x
from 2026.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
AllRing Tech Co. (6187.TWO)
Base case, residual income model. Our key assumptions are:
n Cost of equity (CoE) of 8%, derived from a risk-free rate of 2.0%, beta of 1.0, and a
market risk premium of 6.0%.
n Intermediate growth rate of 16%.
n Cash dividend payout of 65%
n Terminal growth rate of 4.5%.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger-than-expected CoWoS capacity expansion
n Faster-than-expected adoption of silicon photonics

n Market share gains from peers, led by continuous R&D and China's localization trend
Risks to Downside
n Slowdown in CoWoS capacity expansion
n Allring's "WoS" market share declining
n Slowdown in technology migration such as SoIC and silicon photonics adoption

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

矽力*-KY (6415 TT) 
明年營收成長性可望達 30%

永豐觀點 
資料中心與車載為成長主軸，兩者總計明年營收比重可望達 45%。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進：研究部預估矽力杰 2026 年稅後 EPS 10.92 元(原預估 11.95 元)，2027
年稅後 EPS 15.32 元。考量矽力杰資料中心與車載仍將持續帶動營運拓展，研究部
維持買進建議，目標價維持 644 元(42X 2027 年 EPS 評價)。

ESG 評析

向平均得分分別為 B+等與 B 等。

### Page 2

2

矽力杰為電源管理晶片大廠：矽力杰成立於 02/2008，為電源管理 IC 設計公司，
產品線包括 DC/DC 轉換晶片、電池充電管理晶片、過流保護晶片、靜電防護裝置
與 LED 背光驅動晶片、能源監控管理晶片等。矽力杰 3Q26 產品終端應用主要區
分為五大領域，包括消費電子(如平板電腦、STB 與 TV)34%、工業應用(如 LED 照
明、伺服器與安防設備等)37%、資訊產品(如筆電)7%與網路通訊(如路由器、手機
等)7%、車載 15%。國際電源管理晶片設計同業與矽力杰產品類似者為 TI、Maxim、
ON Semi 與 ADI，台系類似的參與廠商則包括致新、茂達等。 
3Q26 營業費用較高：矽力杰公布 3Q26 營收 72.59 億元(+21%QoQ，+52%YoY)，
毛利率 47.58%，稅後獲利 9.09 億元(-42%QoQ，+34%YoY)，稅後 EPS 2.31 元，
財報不如預期，主要是營業費用規模偏高。矽力杰 3Q26 財報細項指標如下：(1) 
營收 72.59 億元(+21%QoQ，+52%YoY)，此係來自資料中心訂單加持、車載上
升(新款晶片推出與市占率提升)、消費電子季增 14%(即便市場需求疲弱，但因公
司新款晶片推出，故反而仍能向上)、資通訊產品下滑(因記憶體報價上漲，推高終
端售價而影響消費力道)。(2) 毛利率由 2Q26 的 47.03%微增至 47.42%，符合公
司原預期。(3) 營業費用 24.78 億元，季增達 19%，主要是銷售費用、資料中心應
用研發費用、3Q26 應屆畢業生招募人力增加。(4) 業外損失約 1100 萬元，相較
2Q26 業外收益 9.58 億元出現明顯差異，主要是前期有加特蘭微電子的再評價增
值利益產生。(5) 存貨 70.36 億元，季增 23%，存貨天數 150 天，達到歷史上緣位
置，公司表示係因封測供給吃緊，導致封測交期拉長(部分達到六個月)，因此 wafer bank
囤積在封測廠，導致庫存水位增加。

4Q26 營收持平到小增，略有不如預期：矽力杰預期(1) 4Q26 營收持平到小增(研
究部原預估 QoQ+5%~6%)，其中營運動能仍以資料中心為成長主軸，不過消費
電子市況相當疲弱(公司推測可能與客戶因應原物料與供應鏈不確定性，而提前出
貨，導致 4Q26 拉貨動能驟減)，包括手機與 TV 均顯弱勢。(2) 毛利率隨產品結構
變化、Gen4 製程平台滲透率提升至 20%，使得毛利率應能優於 3Q26 水準。
(3)4Q26~1H27 營業費用建議以 3Q26 的水準計算。研究部預估矽力杰 4Q26 營
收 74.29 億元(+2%QoQ，+38%YoY)，毛利率 47.84%，稅後獲利 10.5 億元
(+16%QoQ，+30%YoY)，稅後 EPS 2.67 元。 
2027 年營收成長性落於 YoY+20%~+30%上緣，反映成長力道延續：矽力杰維持
預期資料中心、車載與為主要成長推手(兩者 2027 年營收總計 YoY+>50%)，並給
予更明確的營收比重目標值：(1) 資料中心(包括主板/企業級 SSD/光模組)目前營
收比重 17%，2027 年底預計可達 25%。公司資料中心三大項目比重依序為光通
訊>SSD>MB，其中光通訊與 SSD 個別均為 mid-to-high single digit，MB 比重
雖較低，不過主板業務(包括控制晶片與 SPS)的新款晶片目前客戶均已處驗證中，
預計 2027 年將放量(公司表是北美客戶的 SPS 需求快於中國)。公司表示儘管近期
資料中心有諸多因素干擾，不過客戶針對明年訂單需求仍相當穩固。(2) 車載營收
比重可望由現階段 15%，攀升至 2027 年的 20%(原預估 15%~20%)，主要動能
來自於 content value 提升(由 200~200USD 往 270~300USD 邁進，係來自 MCU
與高電壓/大電流晶片出貨)、中國電動車的海外銷售攀升。(3) 公司期待隨 Gen4
製程平台 2027 年滲透率達 50%、Gen5 製程平台量產與產品結構持續往資料中
心、車載布局，對 2027 年毛利率>50%有信心。不過研究部認為矽力杰過往毛利
率容易受到存貨損失調整(因庫齡政策)影響，又製造成本上漲壓力延續，故雖預估
2027 年毛利率趨勢向上，但上升的幅度以較保守的態度推估。

維持買進：研究部預估矽力杰 2026 年營收 255.51 億元(+36%YoY)，毛利率
47.6%，稅後獲利 42.98 億元(+72%YoY)，稅後 EPS 10.92 元(原預估 11.95 元)，
並預估 2027 年營收 338.3 億元(+32%YoY)，毛利率 48.98%，稅後獲利 60.32 億
元(+40%YoY)，稅後 EPS 15.32 元。考量矽力杰資料中心與車載仍將持續帶動營
運拓展，研究部維持買進建議，目標價維持 644 元(42X 2027 年 EPS 評價)。

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Oil Price Dynamics Remain Key to the Outlook
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
Inflation, interest rates and equity market dynamics have changed materially since mid-
August, as widening attacks on Middle East and Russian crude oil and refining capacity
worsened supply and price conditions. Indeed, with the oil market already strained, an
early-September attack on a major Saudi Arabian export pipeline sharply raised concerns

about a protracted and deepening oil crisis. While Middle East crude exports eventually
managed to significantly rebound, increased security risks and freight costs raised
physical crude prices.
Against this backdrop, the spot Brent price surged from $92/barrel in mid-August
back into a $115 to $135/barrel price range until recently. Reflecting unusual market
tightness, other North Sea oil grades were recently trading even higher, up to $160/
barrel, according to the International Energy Agency (IEA). Even as it cooled from $135

to $115/barrel between October 2 and October 20, spot Brent is still almost double
year-ago levels, and so are many diesel prices. The AAA average U.S. retail diesel price
recently hit a new record and was 74% higher year-over-year (YoY) as of October 20.
With insufficient regional supply, the New York Harbor wholesale diesel price is 130%
higher from a year ago, according to the Energy Information Administration (EIA).
Indeed, sharply higher oil prices and a growing diesel shortage have put the global diesel

market under extreme strain. The U.S., for example, has little distillate cushion and
limited spare refining capacity. The distillate inventory (diesel and fuel oil) has decreased
from 37 days of demand coverage in January to just 26 days by early October, according
to the EIA. This 30% drop brought the supply buffer to the bottom of its historical
range. With limited refining capacity and reliance on imports for about 10% of regional
consumption, East Coast distillate inventories are the lowest since 1990, for example,

and are expected to remain below their 5-year average into late 2027, according to EIA
data. More broadly, according to the IEA’s October Oil Market Report (OMR), “oil market
balances are forecast to remain in deficit through the end of the year, with constrained
middle distillate supplies likely to persist well into 2027.”
Thus, although wage growth/labor demand do not appear inflationary, the oil crisis
risks continuing to feed “core” inflation. According to the Federal Reserve’s Beige Book1

covering precisely the mid-August to early-October period of heightened oil-market
turmoil, “higher oil prices permeated all District reports,” with firms noting that fuel
surcharges were passing on increased costs throughout the supply chain. The 22% rise
in airfares over the past year alone contributed around 0.2 percentage point (pp) to the
2.6% 12-month increase in the “core” consumer price index (CPI) for September. Aside
from transportation costs effects, the spike in diesel prices also portends high food

prices with a few-month lag given planting/harvesting cost considerations.
By solidifying expectations for higher Fed interest rates to fight inflation, the surge in oil
prices has contributed to the spike in long-term Treasury yields this year. According to an
October 13, 2026, Empirical Research Partners report, the Iran-war oil crisis is estimated
to have contributed about 0.50 pp to the roughly 2.40 pp increase in the real U.S. long-
term Treasury rate from 2020 to early October (Exhibit 1A). According to the report, this

oil-price effect accounts for “much of the rise in real rates since February 27, 2026...”
On the positive side, while most other underpinnings for higher rates compared to the
pre-pandemic decade are structural (Exhibit 1A), the oil-price-related effect on interest
rates is reversible, implying that a resolution to the current oil crisis has the potential to
help lower both inflation and interest rates, relieving some of the current headwinds to
growth and asset prices.

Indeed, while much of the increase in interest rates since the pandemic reflects stronger,
more normal economic growth, and has thus been accompanied by surging corporate
1 Summary of Commentary on Current Economic Conditions by Federal Reserve District, Federal Reserve, October 2026.
Investment Implications
Oil market conditions remain a swing
factor for the outlook. A shift lower in
oil risk premia and improvement on
the refining front would go a long way

toward easing inflation and interest
rates while bolstering risk-asset
appetite. A gradual increase in Fixed
Income portfolio duration continues to
make sense, in our view.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
earnings and risk-asset prices, worsening oil-market conditions since mid-summer and
associated spike in yields have been accompanied by a loss of momentum in U.S. equity
prices (Exhibit 1B). Given their higher sensitivity to energy price and interest rates shocks,
Small-caps lost relatively more momentum than Large-caps during this period.
The Large-cap rally also faded, though, as oil and interest rate headwinds widened stock-

level return dispersion, with roughly 40% of constituent stocks in bear market territory
(down 20% or more). Still, buoyed by the Technology sector as well as by expectations
of conflict resolution, the market-capitalization weighted S&P 500 Index has mainly
consolidated just below its peak. While the equal-weighted S&P 500 Index stumbled more,
it was just about 3% off its peak as of October 20.
While the economy remains resilient and profit expectations remain strong, persistent

oil market turmoil makes the macro backdrop more fragile. For example, the Beige Book
not only highlights concern about spreading fuel-related inflation, as noted above, but its
language also suggests that economic activity lost some momentum since its mid-July
to late-August report. Overall, it describes growth as slight to moderate, with generally
moderate manufacturing growth except for robust activity in the energy-heavy Dallas
District. Little growth is reported in other sectors overall, with most Districts seeing little

to no change in consumer spending, for example.
Employment was described as stable to little changed, with generally adequate labor supply
except for certain specialized trade workers. Five of twelve Districts note some reports of
businesses cutting staff through attrition, delaying hiring or anticipating less job creation
as a result of AI-driven productivity gains. Most Districts reported that business maintained
a positive outlook for coming months, though “expectations tend to be for slight growth,

weighted toward manufacturing and burdened by persistent uncertainty.”
The September employment and retail sales reports seem to validate the anecdotal Beige
Book evidence. Also, the 0.4% annualized manufacturing production growth over the July to
September period (and 1.1% YoY, according to the Federal Reserve Broad), while welcome,
is hardly an indication of booming conditions. Against a backdrop of rising electricity prices,
wage growth lagging inflation, and higher gasoline prices broadly offsetting this year’s tax

cut benefits, real consumer spending is unlikely to accelerate much absent stronger labor
demand and real wage growth. A resolution to the oil crisis that brings economic relief and
tempers interest rates is thus critical for continued expansion in 2027, in our view.

Investment Implications
Portfolio maintenance is a key
component of successful investing.
It’s time for investors to assess their
portfolios—or look under the hood.
MARKET VIEW
Six Questions Investors Should Be Asking Themselves Right Now
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
After four years of rising equity prices, investors have plenty to celebrate. Despite

inflation scares, geopolitical tensions, trade wars, rising interest rates and breathless
predictions around AI, the S&P 500 has returned some 131% (total returns) since the
bull market in equities commenced in October 2022. Corporate earnings have generally
exceeded expectations; AI has ignited a historic investment boom, and America’s
technology giants have powered the market to new heights.
We believe the bull will rumble on into 2027. We also believe that now is the time for

investors to take stock of their portfolios, since bull markets have a way of changing the
composition of portfolios. In addition, investors should not assume that the investment
strategies that worked well in the past four years will necessarily deliver comparable
results during the next four. Below are six questions investors should be asking
themselves right now:
Number One: How concentrated is my portfolio? Many investors believe they are
invested in the stock market when, in fact, they are invested in a handful of companies—

specifically AI-related names that have soared and dominated the index over the past
few years. Information technology now accounts for roughly 40% of the market cap of
the S&P 500, while the top 10 companies represent a share of 38.4%.
As the Financial Times recently noted, U.S. equity and credit markets have become so
increasingly concentrated in AI-related groups that “more than two-thirds of companies
in the Russell 1000 index of large U.S. groups are linked to AI, either directly or

indirectly or because the technology is becoming embedded in their business models.”2
Meanwhile, Big Technology hyperscalers and companies that are beneficiaries of the AI
spending boom make up about 16% of the U.S. high-grade bond market after a huge
borrowing binge, making the grouping the largest in the investment-grade bond market.2
The upshot: Investors that have been rewarded by concentration need to ask themselves
what percentage of their wealth is tied to the all-encompassing AI trade and consider

what would happen if mega-cap tech leadership falters.
Number Two: How diversified is my portfolio? While concentration asks whether
investors own too much of a few assets, diversification asks whether investors own enough
of different assets that behave differently when economic cycles or financial conditions
change. A portfolio containing hundreds of U.S. Equities via exchange-traded funds, mutual
funds and other assets may appear diversified. But if those companies are overwhelmingly

exposed to the same economic cycle, interest rate environment, or technology theme,
the diversification benefits are diluted. Case in point: While we remain constructive on
Emerging Market (EM) Equities, investors need to understand that a significant driver of
EM returns has come from AI-driven markets like Taiwan and South Korea.
Today, true diversification means considering exposure to hard assets (commodities, real
estate), EM mining and agricultural leaders, EM bonds and currencies, China bio-technology,

Small-cap international, and Private Equity and Hedge Funds for qualified investors.
Number Three: How tax-efficient is my portfolio? Bull markets create wealth—in
addition to tax liabilities. The good news is that after four years of rising equity returns,
investors are sitting on sizable, unrealized capital gains. The bad news: Market gains
can feel like a financial straitjacket, making it difficult to rebalance portfolios without
triggering significant tax liabilities.

Against this backdrop, now is the time for investors to review where the assets are
held—distinguishing between taxable and tax-advantaged accounts, and the attendant tax
ramifications. The appropriate mix depends on individual circumstances, including future
2 “Investors seek shelter from heightened exposure to AI”, The Financial Times, October 6, 2026.

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
withdrawal taxes. It is also time to consider tax-loss strategies, charitable donations of
securities and other tactics and strategies since in the end, the measure of investment
success isn’t simply how much money investors make. It’s how much they keep.
Number Four: Is it time to take profits?* There is a peculiarity to bull markets: The
more money investors make, the more the attendant tax liabilities. Tax liabilities, in turn,

can discourage selling, and the reluctance to sell can leave investors dangerously exposed
to yesterday’s leaders and compound concentration risks. That brings up an emotionally
difficult decision: When should investors sell their leaders or take some chips off the table?
The question is notably relevant today, given four years of substantial Equity gains.
Beyond tax considerations, investors need to understand that profit-taking can create
financial flexibility. Realized after-tax gains can replenish cash reserves, fund near-

term spending needs, reduce leverage or most importantly, provide capital to rebalance
portfolios in other areas of the market. In general, the goal of taking profits isn’t to get
out of the market—it is to ensure that after years of accumulated gains, these proceeds
are redeployed and redistributed across various asset classes that continue to support
and underpin long-term wealth accumulation.
Number Five: How well is my portfolio positioned for a new interest rate

regime? For much of the past quarter century, investors operated in a world of copious
liquidity, declining interest rates and relatively cheap capital. The era of capital abundance
favored stocks over bonds, but that era is over—we are shifting to a world of capital
scarcity, or intense competition for capital and the attendant hike in the cost of capital.
In this world, higher rates weigh the most on long-duration assets. Positioning for this
regime favors companies with strong balance sheets and cash flow over firms with heavy

debt or refinancing needs. High-quality bonds are more attractive in this environment;
sovereign debt risks are concentrated more overseas (i.e., France) than the U.S.
Number Six: How well is my portfolio positioned for the next shock? Shocks
happen. If the past four years have taught investors anything, it is that the unexpected
should be the expected. This decade alone has produced gut-wrenching market moves
related to a global pandemic, a ground war in Europe, the reconfiguration of global

supply chains, epic technological change and a war in the Middle East. Through it all, U.S.
Equities have trended higher but hardly in a linear fashion.
The next shocks could emerge in the global bond market, where mounting government
debt and higher borrowing costs have triggered mounting volatility. It could emerge from
disappointing returns on massive AI investments. Or it could come from a geopolitical
event, another energy shock or any other unforeseen event. Investors can’t predict the

next shock, but they can prepare for it. So now is the time for investors to stress test
their portfolios against a potential shock, examining how a steep downdraft in Equities
or spike in bond yields would affect their retirement plans, liquidity needs and other
financial goals and objectives.
Exhibit 2: Six Questions to Stress Test Your Portfolio.
The six questions What investors should examine Portfolio diagnostic
1. How concentrated am I? Mega-cap technology exposure and overlapping holdings % of portfolio in top 10 stocks

2. Am I truly diversified? Asset allocation, geographic mix and correlated exposures Current equity/bond mix versus target
3. How tax-efficient am I? Unrealized gains, asset location and tax-loss harvesting Embedded capital gains and estimated tax cost of rebalancing
4. Is it time to take profits? Portfolio drift, oversized winners and valuations Positions exceeding target allocation
5. Am I ready for higher interest rates? Duration, leverage, refinancing risks and cash flows Bond duration and exposure to highly leveraged companies

6. Am I prepared for the next shock? Liquidity, downside exposure and portfolio resilience Estimated loss in a 20% equity selloff
Source: Chief Investment Office. Data as of October 22, 2026.
* “Pop the Champagne,” Portfolio Insights, Chief Investment Office, August 2026.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are rising credit spreads signaling about the broader market environment?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
Rising French bond yields with a sharp rise in spreads above German bunds, mixed
with AI buildout concerns, are creating nervousness across the capital markets. This
nervousness centers around the ability to fund deficits, the potential for slower growth

and the future return on the announced capital investment. We expect these concerns
to continue to linger well into next year as growth worries remain in France, bond
yields stay sticky around current levels, and, in the case of AI, investors demand more
compensation for financing.
At this stage, we do not expect the rising spreads in targeted areas to be a precursor
for a material slowdown in economic growth overall. Rather, we believe this is a natural

signal that investors are moving up the quality stack as we move into the middle of
the AI buildout. The bottom line is that rising credit spreads in lower quality corners of
the market are natural along the lifecycle of financing. When free cash flow is called
into question, or when debt financing costs begin to approach the expected return on
investment, investors begin to reprice risk.
We continue to emphasize higher quality balance sheets in corporate and sovereign

credit as well as higher quality leaders in the AI buildout overall. The closer we get to
2027, investors will likely begin to transition from too much exposure to the “buildout”
and not enough exposure to “AI adopters.”

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
SS&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and
services.
S&P 500 Market-cap Weighted Index is a float-adjusted market-capitalization-weighted index, meaning a company's influence and percentage weight in the index are determined by its
total market value (share price multiplied by the number of outstanding shares).

S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is a variation of the traditional S&P 500 where every single company in the index is given the exact same weight—0.2%—regardless of its size or market
capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Industrials Index imposes equal weights on the index constituents included in the S&P 500 that are classified in the GICS® industrials sector.
Russell 1000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of the 1,000 largest publicly traded companies in the United States.

This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase
or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.

oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.
All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.
Nonfinancial assets, such as closely held businesses, real estate, fine art, oil, gas and mineral properties, and timber, farm and ranch land, are complex in nature and involve risks including total

### Page 1

COP31 Preview: Cooperation Amid Fragmentation
COP31, taking place in Antalya, Türkiye from 9 to 20 November 2026, will test whether the ‘Implementation COP’ can
turn climate ambition into finance delivery amid heightened geopolitical tensions. Corporates and investors should
watch for signals that could shape policy and capital allocation, including commitments to grid investment,
electrification, adaptation and carbon markets. We also expect AI’s climate potential and rising electricity demand to

Deutsche Bank
Research
Debbie Jones | Andrew Dixon
S U S T A I N A B I L I T Y  | T H E M A T I C  R E S E A R C H  | O C T O B E R  2 0 2 6
The report outlines the top 10 focus topics, discussions and outcomes for COP31 (see pages 1-3 for a summary)

### Page 2

C O P 3 1  P r ev iew  |  TO P  1 0  (1  o f 3 )
Antalya  - 10 things to watch for at COP31
2 Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Preview  |  October 2026
From 9 to 20 November, ~100,000 participants and nearly 200 Parties are expected at COP31 in Antalya, Türkiye. This is an ‘Implementation COP’ and will focus on climate finance delivery for
mitigation and adaptation commitments. Yes, there will be geopolitical tensions and competitiveness concerns. But, for corporates and investors, the discussions will shape how private capital is

mobilised, and how policies will affect de-risking efforts. We expect electrification efforts will feature prominently, driven by energy security concerns as well as by rising AI-driven power
demand. Furthermore, AI’s ability to help tackle climate change has the potential to play a bigger role in shaping the agenda of future COPs.
01
TÜRKIYE HOSTS, AUSTRALIA LEADS
NEGOTIATIONS
Türkiye and Australia must stay aligned
to drive progress
See page 6
– As host, Türkiye’s Murat Kurum will preside over COP31 and its Action Agenda, which is intended to build cooperation between

willing participants to drive climate action. Australia’s Chris Bowen will run the formal negotiations that will seek consensus (or
lack of objection) on finance delivery and existing climate commitments. Feedback following pre-COP meetings on the
arrangement has been broadly positive, but concern remains over how any potential disagreement will be handled.
– This structure is the result of a compromise after both countries bid to host. Both countries want COP31 to focus on climate

finance implementation and clean-energy growth, however, they remain coal-reliant (in different ways) with no national coal
phase-out date, a criticism likely to continue from climate advocates at COP.
02
CLIMATE FINANCE
Success at COP31 requires notable
progress on mobilising existing
commitments
See page 7
– For COP31 to be successful it will need to deliver agreements to mobilise and channel climate finance. For context, at COP29 in
2024 it was agreed (as part of formal negotiations) that developed nations would commit $300bn in climate finance annually

by 2035, with a call for $1.3tn from all sources. At present, more credible plans are needed to secure existing commitments
and attract broader capital. Note the EU has already stated it will not make new finance pledges at COP31.
– Article 9 contains the main financing provisions of the Paris Agreement and has driven much debate during COP talks.
Developing countries want focus on public funding from developed governments (Article 9.1), while developed countries prefer

to discuss Article 9 in a broader context. This allows private capital to be included. COP30 launched a two-year work
programme on Article 9, but June’s Bonn talks stalled, so COP31 meetings will attempt to reach agreement.
03
ADAPTATION & RESILIENCE
Implications of a 1.5°C overshoot point
to more adaptation financing
See pages 8–9
– The UN has warned that an overshoot of the Paris Agreement’s 1.5°C goal is now unavoidable, at least temporarily. That has

dampened morale for climate supporters ahead of COP but, at the same time, it has drawn more attention to the topic of
adaptation finance, which is set to be on the formal negotiating agenda. Parties appear deadlocked over whether the COP30
goal to triple adaptation finance by 2035 (potential annual target of $120bn) should be explicitly referenced in the final COP31
decision on the Global Goal on Adaptation (GGA). This is important, as anchoring it to the GGA could signal finance flows.

– The EU Commission proposed the Climate Resilience law on 27 October. It needs Parliament and Council approval, but if
approved, member states will be required to run national climate-risk assessments based on a 3°C-by-2100 warming scenario
(i.e. the EU is planning for overshoot). Members would also need to adopt resilience plans, including details of costs and
funding, with The Commission estimating the EU needs €70bn a year for adaptation to 2050, vs. €29bn of current spend.

### Page 3

Antalya  - 10 things to watch for at COP31
3 Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Preview  |  October 2026
04
ELECTRIFICATION
Türkiye to champion voluntary
electrification commitments
See page 10
– Türkiye's voluntary Action Agenda includes a ‘35-by-35’ target to lift electricity’s share of final energy use from ~23% to 35% by
2035, alongside waste, building energy intensity and circularity targets. This sits outside the formal negotiations and commits
only those that volunteer. China and the EU have voiced support, and the timing is right given global energy-security concerns,

though some question the emissions impact if fossil fuels are used to generate the additional electricity.
06
TRADE TENSIONS
Trade’s inclusion in the provisional
agenda is contentious, with the
potential for disagreement over trade to
stall progress on wider agreements
See page 12
– The role of trade at COP is contentious, but China wants it discussed. On 21 October, Vice Minister Li Gao called ‘unilateralism
and protectionism’ a ‘new problem’ undermining collective climate efforts. Specifically, he wants COP31 to discuss the free

flow of green technology. This was widely read as being aimed at US and EU tariffs on Chinese cleantech.
– Despite a COP30 agreement to create three annual trade dialogues in 2026–28 (the first formal space under the UNFCCC to
discuss trade) away from COP, developing countries are still pushing for trade to be a formal negotiating item in Antalya, and it
is on the provisional agenda. They argue that climate-related trade measures such as the CBAM burden their exporters and

question whether they are compatible with UNFCCC principles. The EU meanwhile claims they help prevent carbon leakage,
with developed countries preferring trade discussions to focus on technical information-sharing without duplicating the WTO.
C O P 3 1  P r ev iew  |  TO P  1 0  (2  o f 3 )
07
GEOPOLITICS & MULTILATERALISM
Consensus under strain, but ‘coalitions
of the willing’ are rising
See pages 13–14
– Geopolitical fragmentation and the US exit from the Paris Agreement make consensus, historically central to COP decision-

making, harder among nearly 200 nations. This has encouraged more voluntary action and “coalitions of the willing” to pursue
more ambitious goals. If these strategies deliver more effective progress, their approach will shape future COPs.
05
FOSSIL FUELS
Phase-out likely not on the formal
agenda, but workarounds are emerging
See page 11
– Brazil’s COP30 presidency is to present a fossil fuel transition roadmap separately, outside the UN formal negotiating process.

For perspective, COP28 in Dubai reached consensus on “transitioning away from fossil fuels in energy systems”, but at COP30
in Belém (2025) calls from more than 80 countries for a transition roadmap faced opposition from other nations, so it was
omitted from the final decision and moved to a separate track.
– Santa Marta is a second workaround for the lack of formal agreement on a transition roadmap. The Colombia- and
Netherlands-led process is a ‘coalition of the willing’ of 57 participants (excluding the US, China, India and Russia) agreeing to

voluntary action to move away from fossil fuels faster than consensus allows. At COP31, Colombia and the Netherlands want
to bring the Santa Marta outcomes into the summit. The format is not yet settled, but the Australian and Turkish presidencies
have said they are open to it. The process then heads to a second conference in Tuvalu, co-hosted with Ireland, in 2027.

### Page 4

09
NATURE & CARBON CREDITS
Efforts to boost demand and build
credibility to help scale carbon markets
See page 16
– Carbon credit discussions will centre on government policies to boost demand and corporate engagement, with much of the
formal UN architecture now in place. If carbon markets can scale, the benefits would be wide-ranging (e.g. supporting
emissions targets and providing finance for developing countries). Credibility and greenwashing concerns remain key risks,

however; we expect prices to diverge in favour of high-integrity credits.
– The Tropical Forest Forever Facility (TFFF), launched at COP30, plans a fixed-income portfolio to reward verified forest
conservation. It now has ~$7.8bn of sponsor capital against a $10bn end-2026 target. In our view, fresh COP31 commitments
would signal appetite to fund nature conservation and protection.
10
PACIFIC, OCEANS & SEA-LEVEL RISE
Australia to bring Pacific priorities into

focus
See page 17
– Australia asked for a Pacific pre-COP in return for ceding the hosting. It has promised to keep championing Pacific interests at
COP31. The Taku Pakasoa Declaration was adopted at that pre-COP on 6 October. ‘Taku Pakasoa’ is Tuvaluan for strength
found in unity and collective action. It is a joint call to keep 1.5°C alive, calling for action on climate finance, resilience and
oceans. Its intention is to give the Pacific a shared position going into COP31 and signal its policy priorities to other blocs.

Other countries are invited to sign on. The climate-ocean nexus will also be a COP31 focus, including investable solutions.
COP31 should indicate what COP can deliver in a more fragmented geopolitical world. Key questions? Can the current structure help mobilise meaningful finance and investment, and do
smaller coalitions and voluntary commitments deliver meaningful outcomes? Answers here could shape future COP agendas.  Looking ahead, Ethiopia will host COP32 in 2027. There, we

expect a continued focus on delivering on commitments and improving access to climate finance, reflecting African priorities. COP33 then falls to Asia-Pacific in 2028, with China
reportedly considering a bid and South Korea potentially interested.
We should note that the second Global Stocktake, the Paris Agreement’s five-yearly review of collective progress, begins at COP31 and concludes at COP33, with a technical assessment
(2027) informing the final negotiations. Its outcome should inform countries’ next national climate plans (NDCs, their emissions-reduction pledges), due in 2030.

08
AI & CLIMATE
AI’s power demand in focus, with the
Antalya pledge calling for low emissions
infrastructure
See page 15
– The Antalya AI Pledge invites governments and companies to commit voluntarily to sustainable infrastructure and
environmental transparency. Its call for zero- and low-emissions energy infrastructure leaves room for nuclear power,
prompting discussion of its role in meeting data-centre demand alongside renewables. While we see binding commitments as

unlikely, watch for corporate power commitments and technology partnerships.
– We expect AI’s climate potential and rising electricity demand to feature prominently at COP31, prompting debate over data-
centre energy use. We also expect AI to help delegations manage COP’s heavy paperwork, from reviewing negotiating texts
and submissions to comparing successive draft decisions. This should help negotiating teams prepare more effectively and
make decisions faster, particularly smaller delegations with limited staff and resources.

### Page 6

TO P I C  1 | COP31’s UNIQUE STRUCTURE
COP31’s unique dual structure will need to bridge divides among negotiating groups and build consensus
As host, Türkiye’s Murat Kurum will preside over COP31 and its Action Agenda. Australia’s Chris Bowen will run the formal negotiations. This dual structure is the result of a compromise after
both countries bid to host. Feedback post pre-COP meetings on the unique arrangement has been broadly positive, but concern remains over how any disagreement between the two countries

would be handled should it occur. Both countries clearly want this year’s COP to focus on climate finance implementation and clean-energy growth.
A unique leadership structure
COP31 will take place in Antalya, Türkiye. The setup is unusual, emerging as part of a deal
between Türkiye and Australia after both countries bid to host the event. What began as a
compromise could become an advantage, provided the two countries remain aligned. Their
differing diplomatic relationships could help them bridge divides between negotiating groups

and build common ground on climate action and finance.
How it will work
Murat Kurum, Türkiye’s Minister of the Environment, will serve as COP31 President, overseeing
the conference and Action Agenda. Australia’s Minister for Climate Change and Energy, Chris
Bowen, will serve as President of Negotiations, including drafting final texts, with authority to
steer government-to-government talks. Pre-COP preparations have reportedly been positive.
They will, however, both likely face criticism from climate advocates asking for a plan to shift

from fossil fuels, notably coal, given efforts to position themselves as global climate leaders.
The Action Agenda includes targets for electrification, waste, buildings and circularity
This voluntary Agenda proposes 2035 targets of a 35% electricity share of final energy use (up
from 23% currently), halving projected municipal waste growth, cutting building energy
intensity by at least 25% and reaching at least 15% circular material use. While pledges are

non-binding, they can signal policy and decisions that could support infrastructure investment.
Formal negotiations to focus on implementing existing commitments
Formal negotiations seek consensus (or lack of objection) on implementation pathways. The
key need is guidance on how agreed climate-finance commitments will be funded and
delivered, and on countries’ roles. Adaptation will also be a focus, with countries aiming to
reach agreement on how to advance the Global Goal on Adaptation after discussions stalled

earlier this year. These formal talks remain separate from the voluntary Action Agenda.
Sources: COP31 Action Agenda, IEA and Eco-Business

### Page 7

TO P I C  2 | C L I MATE F I N AN C E
Climate finance delivery will be a central test for COP31
June’s Bonn preparatory talks reportedly made little progress and exposed persistent finance divisions, leaving COP31 to turn both the $300bn and $1.3tn-by-2035 goals into a more credible
delivery plan. This is a big ask and requires clearer public/private roles, consistent tracking mechanisms, more concessional finance, risk-sharing and guarantees that can help reduce the cost of

capital. New large-scale finance commitments appear unlikely, with the EU already stating it will not make new pledges.

$300 billion a year by 2035
COP31 focus: what counts and who contributes. This amount

contributes towards the $1.3tn NCQG target from all actors.
Context: Developed countries agreed to lead mobilisation of at least
$300bn a year by 2035, counting public finance and the private
finance it mobilises; no country has a fixed share.
At COP31: Focus turns to how to deliver. Developing countries are
pushing for the development of action plans to chart a pathway to
$300bn and for the Article 9 work programme to be included on the
formal agenda (with emphasis on Article 9.1 – which explicitly covers

developed country finance provision).
Adaptation finance
COP31 focus: embedding and tracking the tripling pledge
Context: COP30 called for adaptation finance to at least triple by
2035, yet this was relatively vague; it did not specify a base year or a
target figure (some have suggested $120bn, i.e. 3x the 2025 goal).
At COP31: Parties need to resolve disagreement on whether to
anchor the pledge in the Global Goal on Adaptation (GGA) and put a
finer point on the agreed Belém Adaptation Indicators that will track

it, after disagreement stalled the June Bonn talks.
Loss and Damage Fund
COP31 focus: guidance, replenishment and access
Why it matters: The Fund is the only dedicated channel for
irreversible climate impacts, yet it has no annual funding target and
resources remain far below demand.
At COP31: Focus will be on its long-term funding strategy, with the
first replenishment cycle set to begin in 2027. $342m made available
in the first funding window, which offers $5–20m grants with at least

half reserved for SIDS and LDCs; but the voluntary nature of
payments, disputes over who pays and sensitivity around
“compensation” make major new pledges in Antalya unlikely.

Grants vs loans

Developing countries want grants (especially for

The US is pushing MDBs (e.g. the World Bank), to drop

### Page 8

TO P I C  3 | AD AP TATI O N  &  R ES I L I EN C E (1  o f 2 )
Adaptation will be one of the most important, and more divisive, negotiating topics
Adaptation is also set to be on the formal negotiating agenda at COP31. UN warnings that an overshoot of the Paris Agreement’s 1.5°C goal is now unavoidable (at least temporarily), have
dampened morale for climate activists. As it relates to the COP agenda on adaptation, parties appear deadlocked on whether the COP30 goal to triple adaptation finance by 2035 should be

included in the Global Goal on Adaptation (GGA) text. This is important, as anchoring it to the GGA could signal to investors the scale and timing of future public and blended finance flows.
The role of adaptation in the COP process
The Global Goal on Adaptation (GGA) was established under the Paris Agreement to enhance
adaptive capacity, strengthen resilience and reduce vulnerability to climate change. At COP30
last year, countries adopted 59 indicators (still being refined) to measure progress against the

goal. They also agreed a goal to triple adaptation finance by 2035. The pledge was vague: it set
neither a concrete figure nor a baseline year (a potential annual target of $120bn would be
triple the previous goal of c.$40bn by 2025).
Why GGA talks were deadlocked at the June climate meetings
Finance was the main sticking point at the June Bonn meetings. Developing countries wanted
the COP30 goal to triple adaptation finance by 2035 referenced in the GGA text; countries

including Canada, Norway and Japan opposed. Parties also split on whether the indicators task
force should be expert- or party-led, so no GGA text was agreed and the decision passes to
COP31. Moving the Adaptation Fund to serve the Paris Agreement exclusively (funded by a 5%
share of proceeds from Article 6.4 credits) stalled in Bonn over the composition of its board;
talks will resume at COP31.
What is needed at COP31 to unlock adaptation progress

Adaptation is on the provisional COP31 agenda, including the tripling adaptation finance goal
(though this may get blocked). Some parties want it explicitly referenced in the final COP31
GGA decision; this should become clearer when the agenda is adopted as COP31 opens on 9
Nov. At issue: the tripling pledge still lacks a baseline and named contributors. At COP30,
developed nations resisted new finance obligations, while the EU and many others pushed for

a roadmap to transition away from fossil fuels, which a group of mostly oil- and gas-producing
countries blocked. The result was a looser tripling goal and no agreed fossil-fuel roadmap.
Developing countries are unlikely to give ground, as adaptation relies far more on public
funding than mitigation, so a repeat of COP30’s finance vs. mitigation debate is plausible.
Sources: United Nations, UNEP, Carbon Brief, European Parliament (COP30 outcomes briefing, December 2025)

### Page 9

TO P I C  3 | AD AP TATI O N  &  R ES I L I EN C E (2  o f 2 )
The EU’s proposed Climate Resilience Law plans for a 3°C world, while reiterating commitment to net zero 2050
The EU Commission’s recently proposed Climate Resilience Law would set a reference global warming trajectory of 3°C, providing a benchmark for member states’ adaptation planning. The EU
states it remains committed to reaching net zero by 2050, but some at COP will likely view the proposal as accepting overshoot. With the EU also pointing to an estimated need for EUR70bn in

annual adaptation investment, this provides an interesting backdrop as COP31 seeks to resume adaptation negotiations which stalled earlier this year, primarily due to finance.
What the Commission proposed
On 27 October the Commission presented its EU Framework for Climate Resilience, anchored
by a proposed European Climate Resilience Law. With current global policy commitments
pointing to around 2°C of warming by 2050 and 3°C by 2100, the law would set a common

reference trajectory of 1.5°C in 2030, 2°C in 2050 and 3°C in 2100, with this regarded as the
“baseline assumption”. Member states would be required to use it in national climate risk
assessments and adopt a single national resilience plan by end-2030.
Why it matters: the EU is planning for overshoot
The EU highlighted warnings that overshoot of the 1.5°C goal is now “unavoidable” (even if just
temporarily) as necessitating policy action, even as the EU says it is still committed to climate

neutrality by 2050. The Commission links the plan to Europe’s hottest summer on record and
estimates annual economic losses from climate extremes of €50bn over the past four years,
adding that output losses caused by climate change go far beyond this. It says EU adaptation
needs are ~€70bn a year through to 2050, with Europe warming at 2x the global rate.
What it means for COP31
The proposal demonstrates that warnings on the implications of a significant overshoot of the

Paris Agreement global warming limits are moving from scientific theory to real government
policy. While the opening line of the new EU framework emphasized it remains committed to
reaching net zero by 2050, some may view a 3°C planning baseline as accepting overshoot.
The headline €70bn annual EU adaptation finance requirement also provides an interesting
backdrop heading into Global Goal on Adaptation talks given they are stalled largely due to

disagreement over explicit links to finance in the GGA text. For context, the UNEP estimates
annual adaptation needs for developing countries of $310–365bn by 2035. Ultimately, we
expect adaptation focus to continue growing as warming impacts are increasingly felt and
societal calls for relief grow louder. Corporates will need to consider potential strategic
impacts, while some investors have already begun raising their baseline warming forecasts.

### Page 10

TO P I C  4 | EL EC TR I F I C ATI O N
Türkiye is championing a voluntary pledge to lift electrification from 23% to 35% by 2035
Electrification is expected to be at the centre of this “implementation COP”, targeting a rise in electricity’s share of global final energy use from 23% to 35% by 2035. We expect support for this
action given it is being positioned alongside energy security, but we also expect discussion and debate over how it is delivered (e.g., the role of renewables, nuclear and natural gas), alongside

concerns over clean tech dependency in supply chains. China and the EU have expressed support but not yet committed.
Some may brand this the electrification COP
The Action Agenda pledge aims to raise electricity's share of global final energy from 23%
today to 35% by 2035. The pledge was launched in Bonn this past June and formalised as the
"35-by-35 Global Electrification Pledge" in New York on 21 September.
China and the EU have signaled support, but have not yet signed the pledge

Electrification fits modern day energy-security goals making it a viable target for this year’s
COP. China’s cleantech dominance means it has a further incentive to support initiatives that
seek to increase the pace of global electrification. It also aligns closely with China's existing
development trajectory. While the 35% goal is based on Paris-aligned pathways supported by
IEA and IRENA analysis, the numerical target itself does not require the additional electricity to

come from non fossil fuel sources. This has invited some criticism, though it may make it
easier for some nations to sign the non-binding pledge.
Alignment with the voluntary renewables pledge
Recall that COP28 called for tripling global renewable power capacity by 2030 in COP28’s
formal Global Stocktake decision, equivalent to around 11.2 TW. IRENA’s September 2026
assessment puts capacity at 5.15 TW at end-2025. Record additions of 693 GW in 2025 remain

below the 1,200 GW needed annually in 2026–30. The 35-by-35 pledge complements this
supply goal by shifting transport, buildings and industry towards electricity, enabling
renewable power to replace fossil fuels. Its emissions benefit still depends on cleaner
generation.
We view the goal positively, but delivery needs grid enhancements.
IRENA counts ~2,500 GW of renewable, storage and large-load projects awaiting connection
and says annual investment to strengthen grid infrastructure and enhance system flexibility

must rise from ~$500bn in 2025 to around $1tn through 2035, and $1.2tn thereafter. That
said, signatories signal support, but domestic delivery plans will determine investment.
Sources: UNFCCC, COP31 Presidency, IEA, IRENA

TO P I C  5   |   F O S S I L  F U EL S
The fossil fuel transition roadmap track is growing outside the formal COP process
COP30 failed to agree a fossil fuel transition roadmap, despite support from more than 80 countries. Brazil’s COP30 presidency is therefore developing a roadmap outside formal UN
negotiations to be presented ahead of COP31. Separately, Colombia and the Netherlands convened a ‘coalition of the willing’ of 57 countries in Santa Marta in April 2026 to explore practical

steps towards a fossil fuel phase-out. This parallel forum allows willing countries to cooperate without needing consensus from all COP parties.
Sources: Carbon Brief, Santa Marta conference report
